So... that would be about a year and seven months ago now. Yeah, right around then is when it all started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and seven months where you felt extremely down or depressed?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've felt extremely down or depressed. It's more just... empty, I guess. Like I said, I just don't feel much of anything. But I haven't had any periods where I couldn't get out of bed because I was so sad, or anything like that. Just this constant numbness.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've lost interest, exactly. I mean... I still do them. I haven't stopped doing the things I used to like. I still go through the motions with my wife on the weekends, and I keep up with my normal routines. It's just that, like I said, I don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. So I guess the interest to do them is still there, but the actual feeling just isn't.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My weight has stayed pretty much the same. I mean, maybe a pound or two here and there, but nothing significant. My clothes all still fit the same as they did before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. It's... it's not sadness. Like I was saying before, it's really just that numbness. I don't feel sad, I just don't feel much at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still look forward to things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I look forward to much of anything right now. Like I said about feeling numb, I just kind of get through the week. There isn't really anything on the calendar that makes me feel excited or... or eager for it to happen. I just take it one day at a time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Honestly, that's a big part of what brings me in. I just don't feel real happiness or... closeness anymore. Even when my wife tries to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routines, or if something at work goes really well, I just don't feel anything from it. I can't seem to find pleasure in any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, that's exactly it. That's really the main thing that brought me in to see you. I just can't seem to feel any real happiness or closeness to anyone. Like I mentioned with my wife trying to get us back into our old Sunday routines, or even if a project at work goes perfectly... I just don't get any good feelings from it anymore. I'm just completely unable to enjoy those things now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed. It's not a sad feeling, really. It's more just... empty. I don't feel down in the dumps or like crying or anything like that. It's just that lack of feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicidal thoughts over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nothing like that. I don't have any thoughts about wanting to die or... hurting myself or anything. Like I said, it's really just that empty feeling, but I definitely don't want to end my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is mild or has it significantly impacted your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's definitely had a significant impact. It's not mild at all. At work, I've actually had to hand off floor responsibilities that I always used to manage myself. And at home, it's taking a toll on my marriage. It's just that, like I said, I don't get any enjoyment out of it anymore. So I guess the interest to do them is still there, but the actual feeling just isn't.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My weight has stayed pretty much the same. I mean, maybe a pound or two here and there, but nothing significant. My clothes all still fit the same as they did before.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. It's... it's not sadness. Like I was saying before, it's really just that numbness. I don't feel sad, I just don't feel much at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you still look forward to things that are coming up?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I look forward to much of anything right now. Like I said about feeling numb, I just kind of get through the week. There isn't really anything on the calendar that makes me feel excited or... or eager for it to happen. I just take it one day at a time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Honestly, that's a big part of what brings me in. I just don't feel real happiness or... closeness anymore. Even when my wife tries to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routines, or if something at work goes really well, I just don't feel anything from it. I can't seem to find pleasure in any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, that's exactly it. That's really the main thing that brought me in to see you. I just can't seem to feel any real happiness or closeness to anyone. Like I mentioned with my wife trying to get us back into our old Sunday routines, or even if a project at work goes perfectly... I just don't get any good feelings from it anymore. I'm just completely unable to enjoy those things now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed. It's not a sad feeling, really. It's more just... empty. I don't feel down in the dumps or like crying or anything like that. It's just that lack of feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicidal thoughts over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Nothing like that. I don't have any thoughts about wanting to die or... hurting myself or anything. Like I said, it's really just that empty feeling, but I definitely don't want to end my life.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is mild or has it significantly impacted your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's definitely had a significant impact. It's not mild at all. At work, I've actually had to hand off floor responsibilities that I always used to manage myself. And... well, I had an interview for a regional director position, and I just lost my composure completely. It was bad enough that I actually just walked out of the room. And at home, it's taking a toll on my marriage. It feels really strained because, like I said, I'm there physically, but I'm not really participating in our life together.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your empty feeling is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's severe. It's really the main reason I'm here. I just... I don't feel real happiness or closeness anymore. Even when things go well at work, or when my wife tries to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routines... I just feel nothing. It's completely flat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. hot metal can throw me right back to seeing him on the floor and hearing everyone yelling for help.\n- key: S070\n  name: Trauma_Avoidance\n  description: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I avoid that production line whenever I can, send someone else to walk it, and shut down conversations when people start talking about the rollout or the investigation.\n- key: S071\n  name: Trauma_Related_Amnesia\n  description: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and trying to help are broken up and partly just gone.\n- key: S072\n  name: Negative_Beliefs\n  description: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: S075\n  name: Social_Detachment\n  description: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.\n  manifestation: At home I sit through family meals like I'm behind glass, hearing people talk and laugh but feeling like I don't really belong in the room with them.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.\n  manifestation: I snap at operators, supervisors, and my wife over small questions, and there have been times I've slammed a clipboard or barked at someone before I even knew I was angry.\n- key: S079\n  name: Hypervigilance\n  description: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: On the floor I'm constantly checking gauges, exits, people's hands, and where the emergency stops are, and even in meetings I pick a seat where I can see the door.\n- key: S080\n  name: Exaggerated_Startle_Response\n  description: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.\n  manifestation: If a forklift backs up, a door bangs, or a plant alarm chirps, my whole body jumps like something is exploding, and it can take me several minutes before my hands stop shaking.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I've had to hand off floor responsibilities I used to manage myself, and I lost my composure during a regional director interview badly enough that I walked out. At home, my marriage feels strained because I'm present physically but not really participating in our life together.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since the retrofit accident about 1 year and 7 months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / manager / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S076\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities is mild or has it significantly impacted your daily life?\n\nPatient: It's definitely had a significant impact. It's not mild at all. At work, I've actually had to hand off floor responsibilities that I always used to manage myself. And... well, I had an interview for a regional director position, and I just lost my composure completely. It was bad enough that I actually just walked out of the room. And at home, it's taking a toll on my marriage. It feels really strained because, like I said, I'm there physically, but I'm not really participating in our life together.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your empty feeling is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's severe. It's really the main reason I'm here. I just... I don't feel real happiness or closeness anymore. Even when things go well at work, or when my wife tries to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routines... I just feel nothing. It's completely flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a sad or depressed mood?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not sadness, exactly. I don't feel down or... depressed in that way.
